Net sales, 2011-02-15→2011-03-01 vs 2011-02-01→2011-02-15 (equal 14-day periods), with stock_code breakdown

report_1d339c6f5757ca9a41f51bcf34fdddcdd9fb97ff68d44b0bf816af9877f6855e
Dataset ds_1f8144bb8670bcad07cde082 · GBP · 2026-11-25T07:10:27.168Z

数字事实来自下列已保存证据。模型叙述未经独立核实，贡献拆解不证明因果。

解释（待核实）

Dataset ds_1f8144bb8670bcad07cde082 covers 2010-12-01 08:26 to 2011-12-09 12:50 (source local wall time, no timezone inferred), so both requested 14-day windows are fully inside coverage. Policy retail-gbp-v1, currency GBP: net_sales is signed eligible quantity × unit_price on invoice-line grain, restricted to unit_price > 0, excluding cancellation invoices with nonnegative quantity, and preserving duplicate source lines; it is explicitly not cash collected. Quality flags noted: 135,080 unknown customers, 1,454 missing descriptions, 2,517 nonpositive prices, 10,624 negative quantities, 0 inconsistent cancellations, 0 invalid required fields. Comparison (equal_days, 14 days each): net_sales rose from the baseline window to the current window, with a positive delta (see evidence ev_b720f3f301cf89ee1cfdde957fe846dfbc9c06d3ccae11fc34a28d7088e5f5c1 for exact figures). Breakdown by stock_code (top 12 plus OTHER, ev_e70330bd825d9ef320affd404d9410f6e4af7941edda7d6f093c2b1da0e5102b): the change is not concentrated in the top 12 codes. The OTHER bucket (2,405 groups) carries the largest single positive contribution and is larger than the net total change, meaning the top-12 codes collectively offset part of the OTHER increase. Within the top 12, the largest negative contributions come from AMAZONFEE (a fee/adjustment code, not a merchandise item, moving from zero to a negative amount) and several numeric product codes that fell sharply; the largest positive contributions come from a different set of numeric product codes. Breakdown totals reconcile exactly to the comparison totals, and the tool marks the breakdown as descriptive, not causal. Interpretation limits: net_sales includes bookkeeping and return adjustments, so part of the movement reflects fee/return postings rather than underlying demand. The AMAZONFEE line in particular is an adjustment-type code, so its contribution should not be read as a product-level sales decline. No causal claim is made.

待验证假设

  • The AMAZONFEE negative contribution may reflect a fee or adjustment posting recorded in the current window rather than a change in merchandise demand; this is a bookkeeping artifact hypothesis, not established.
  • The broad-based positive movement in the OTHER bucket (2,405 codes) may indicate a general uplift in order volume or basket size across many small-volume products rather than a few large items.
  • Sharp declines in specific numeric product codes (e.g. 21623, 22693, 22189) may reflect stock availability, end-of-life, or one-off bulk purchases in the baseline window; requires order-level or inventory data to test.
  • Because unknown customers still contribute revenue and 135,080 rows lack customer IDs, some of the change may come from unattributed transactions; customer-level attribution would be needed to test this.

SQL、绑定参数与原始结果
WITH periods AS (SELECT 'baseline' period, ?::TIMESTAMP a, ?::TIMESTAMP b UNION ALL SELECT 'current', ?::TIMESTAMP, ?::TIMESTAMP)
      SELECT p.period, coalesce(sum(quantity::DECIMAL(18,0) * unit_price),0)::VARCHAR value, count(DISTINCT CASE WHEN quantity > 0 AND NOT starts_with(upper(invoice_no),'C') THEN invoice_no END)::VARCHAR orders
      FROM periods p LEFT JOIN facts f ON invoice_time >= p.a AND invoice_time < p.b AND (unit_price > 0 AND NOT (starts_with(upper(invoice_no),'C') AND quantity >= 0)) GROUP BY p.period ORDER BY p.period

SQL、绑定参数与原始结果
SELECT stock_code dimension, coalesce(sum(quantity::DECIMAL(18,0) * unit_price),0)::VARCHAR value, CASE WHEN invoice_time < ?::TIMESTAMP THEN 'baseline' ELSE 'current' END period
      FROM facts WHERE (unit_price > 0 AND NOT (starts_with(upper(invoice_no),'C') AND quantity >= 0)) AND ((invoice_time >= ?::TIMESTAMP AND invoice_time < ?::TIMESTAMP) OR (invoice_time >= ?::TIMESTAMP AND invoice_time < ?::TIMESTAMP)) GROUP BY dimension, period ORDER BY dimension, period
